Hmm, actually, let me think about O10 more carefully. Two thermostats of the same make, one set to 18 and one to 22. Are they one kind or two? Under (K): two components are of one kind on C when there's a bijection of footprints under which their signatures coincide. The signature of component j is {(a,b,L_j(a,b)) : (a,b) ∈ C}. If the set-point is part of the component's relation L_j (i.e., baked in), then the two thermostats have different L_j at the baseline: one keeps 18, the other22. Their signatures differ → two kinds. If the set-point is a port value (an input), then L_j is the same function of the set-point input, and the two differ only in valuation → one kind.

Which is it? The theory says roles are derived from admitted edits: "A port v is an input under A when A contains an edit that sets v directly." If the contract admits setting the dial (which is physically possible - you can turn the dial), then the set-point is a port and the thermostats are one kind. The case doesn't state the contract. But - "The physical theory fixes which changes are possible at all. A contract is a declared subset of those." And non-vacuity: "every physically admitted edit excluded from C is excluded by a stated scope, not silently."

Hmm, so if a contract excludes the set-point edit, that exclusion must be stated. The case states no exclusions. Hmm, but the case also states no contract at all.

The fixed verdict: "Rosa is right about what the devices are." - i.e., one kind. And "Emil's difference matters only when the question is why this room is warm" - i.e., two kinds is question-relative.

Under the theory: on any contract that includes set-point edits (the natural contract for "what the devices are"), one kind → Rosa right. On a contract that fixes the set-points... but such a contract would need a stated scope. Hmm.

Actually, here's the thing: the theory says "Two components no admitted change can separate are one kind at that level, whatever labels anyone attaches to them." What admitted change separates the two thermostats? Changing the set-point! If I set thermostat 1 from 18 to 22, it then behaves like thermostat 2. So no admitted change separates them as KINDS - they're in different STATES. Hmm wait, does the set-point edit separate their signatures or show they're the same?

The signature is the set of relations under the contract's edits. If the contract includes "set the dial to x" for various x, then both thermostats respond identically to that edit: L_j(set-dial-to-x, b) = "holds temperature x" for both j. So their signatures coincide → one kind. If the contract does NOT include the dial edit (say, the contract only includes ambient-temperature perturbations), then... both thermostats still respond the same way to ambient perturbations (deviation from set-point is corrected)! Hmm, actually their relations differ: L_1 = "restore to 18" and L_2 = "restore to 22". Under an ambient perturbation edit, their relations report different outputs (18 vs 22). So their signatures differ → two kinds on that contract.

So: contract with dial edits → one kind (Rosa). Contract with only ambient edits and the set-point baked into L → two kinds (Emil). The case doesn't fix the contract. So the theory's finding depends on the contract → the deciding matter (the contract) is an input the case leaves unstated → SILENT.
